In most cases, the **dumpster enclosure is private property**, owned by the **commercial plaza's landlord or management company**. - The businesses in the plaza typically **lease** the space and may have access rights to the dumpster. - Unauthorized use may be considered **trespassing or illegal dumping** depending on the property rules. 2. **Municipally Owned (City Property)** - If the plaza has an agreement with the **city for waste management**, the enclosure **might be managed by the city** but still remain **on private land**. - Public access is usually **restricted**, meaning the dumpster is **not public property** even if a city waste service collects from it. 3. **Easements or Shared Agreements** - Some commercial properties have **easements** or **agreements** with the city allowing **public access to certain facilities**, but this does not necessarily mean the enclosure itself is city-owned. ### **How to Determine Ownership** - **Check Property Records**: County property appraiser’s website may show parcel ownership. - **Review Lease Agreements**: Businesses in the plaza might have terms regarding the dumpster. - **Contact Property Management**: They can clarify if the enclosure is private. - **City Waste Management**: If the city services the dumpster, they might confirm if they own or just service it.

For detailed information about property ownership and specific facilities, you can consult the **Miami-Dade County Property Appraiser's** records.

Below is a comprehensive overview of the most recent statistics and trends: ### **Overall Homeless Population** - **2023 Data:** On a single night in January 2023, 653,104 individuals were experiencing homelessness across the U.S., marking a 12.1% increase from the previous year. citeturn0search0 - **2024 Data:** By January 2024, this number rose to 771,480, an 18% increase from 2023, representing approximately 23 out of every 10,000 people in the country. citeturn0search3 ### **Demographic Breakdown** - **Children and Families:** In 2024, nearly 150,000 children experienced homelessness, reflecting a 33% increase from the previous year.

Family homelessness saw a nearly 40% rise, particularly in cities impacted by an influx of migrants. citeturn0news10 - **Veterans:** Homelessness among veterans decreased by 8% in 2024, reaching its lowest number on record at 32,882 individuals. citeturn0search3 - **Older Adults:** Approximately 21% of people experiencing homelessness in 2023 were older adults. citeturn0search8 ### **Geographical Distribution** - **States with Highest Homeless Populations in 2024:** - **California:** 187,084 individuals - **New York:** 158,019 individuals - **Florida:** 30,000 individuals - **Washington:** Data not specified citeturn0search3 - **Per Capita Rates:** New York, Vermont, and Oregon had the highest per-capita rates of homelessness in 2023. citeturn0search2 ### **Shelter Statistics** - **Sheltered vs.

Unsheltered:** In January 2024, nearly 500,000 people were staying in shelters or transitional housing, while 274,224 were unsheltered, sleeping in places like sidewalks, transit stations, or abandoned buildings. citeturn0search3 - **Emergency and Transitional Shelters:** Between 2018 and 2022, nearly 327,000 people in the U.S. experiencing homelessness lived in shelters, accounting for 0.1% of the U.S. population. citeturn0search5 ### **Factors Contributing to Homelessness** - **Affordable Housing Crisis:** Rising housing costs, inflation, and stagnating wages have significantly contributed to the increase in homelessness. citeturn0search0 - **Natural Disasters:** Events like the 2023 Maui wildfire led to sudden increases in homelessness, with over 5,200 people staying in emergency shelters in Hawaii on the night of the count. citeturn0news10 - **Influx of Migrants:** Cities such as Denver, Chicago, and New York City reported significant increases in family homelessness due to new immigrants and asylum seekers entering shelters. citeturn0news10 ### **Trends Over Time** - **First-Time Homelessness:** From 2019 to 2023, the number of people entering emergency shelters for the first time increased by more than 23%. citeturn0search0 - **Unsheltered Homelessness:** Since 2016, there has been an annual increase in overall homelessness, unsheltered homelessness, and chronic homelessness. citeturn0search0 These statistics underscore the multifaceted nature of homelessness in the U.S., influenced by economic, social, and environmental factors.
